========== FILE: ./.agents/GEMINI.md ==========

# Project Description

- `PLUS-ULTRA` is our project build to give a user-facing floating desktop assistant that starts with the user's
  session, displays a compact top-center visual presence, manages reminders and
  recurring scripts, manage processes in os (OS-CONCEPT), File management(DBMS-CONCEPT).

# Project Rules & Guidelines

- **Privacy Constraint**: Never read, search, list, or modify any files or directories inside `.my/`. This folder is strictly reserved for the user.
- **Secrets**: Never read, print, create, or edit `.env`. Add new variable NAMES to `.env.sample` with placeholders. Never hardcode credentials or log secrets.
- **Initialization and Techstack**: Load [init-and-techstack.md](./rules/init-and-techstack.md) before the first implementation task and never scan this file after that.
- **Decision Making**: Always consult [ask-before-assuming.md](./rules/ask-before-assuming.md) when multiple technical approaches or designs are viable.
- **Coding Conventions**: Follow [coding-style.md](./rules/coding-style.md) for naming conventions, type hints, and PEP 8 compliance.
- **Project Structure Idea**: Follow [genral-project-structure.md](./rules/genral-project-structure.md) as the codebase expands.

# Python and Linux conventions

- Target the Linux desktop and use Python 3.12+ unless the project explicitly changes
  its support policy.
- Use `uv` for environment creation, dependency management, locking, and running
  project commands. Keep dependency declarations in `pyproject.toml` and commit
  `uv.lock` once the project is initialized.
- Prefer the standard library for platform-neutral code. Isolate Linux-specific
  integration behind small, testable adapters.
- Keep long-running work off the UI thread and make shutdown/cancellation explicit.

# Validation & Testing

Before Phase Documentation Edits and Phase Completion Steps(ReadyToCommit), follow these:
- Run targeted tests first, then the project test suite when practical.
- Every phase ships tests for the happy path, edge cases (empty, missing, very long, unicode) and failure paths (corrupt file, permission denied, constraint violation, interrupted write). Run them with `uv run pytest`.
- For what pytest cannot reach (real Wayland window, tray, notify-send), provide a smoke script or `--smoke-test` under xvfb plus a manual checklist.
- The gate report lists the cases tested, the commands run with their output, and what was NOT verified. "All cases" means every acceptance criterion of the phase plus its error paths.
- Before considering a change complete, run the configured formatter, linter, and
  type checker if they exist.
- Document manual Linux desktop checks for startup behavior, display placement,
  notifications, permissions, and graceful shutdown.

# Documentation Management

After every phase, update the relevant `docs/*.md` files as follows:
- Especially `README.md`, to reflect :
  - Short description
  - Project Folder Structure
  - Project Tech-Stack
  - Project Dependencies
- `.docs/api.md` with new API's and modification.
- `.docs/progress-log.md` with:
  - what is going to implemented.
  - what is implemented in curr-phase.
  - what were the issues we face with solution
  - Important Notes / Misscellaneus INFO !
- `.docs/architecture.MD` with the below format:
  - `# Project Folder Structure`
  - `# Project Tech-Stack`
  - `# Project Dependencies`
- `.docs/commands.md` with newly created testing commands, CLI flags, and manual verification procedures.
**Note**: Keep documentation concise, uniform and accurate.

========== FILE: ./.agents/rules/ask-before-assuming.md ==========
---
trigger: always_on
description: Ask the user before making ambiguous or preference-dependent choices, and pause at phase gates
---

# Ask Before Assuming

- Never silently assume the user's intended choice when multiple reasonable options exist.
- When a decision materially affects the result and the user's preference is unknown, ask the user before proceeding.
- Take Feedback frpm User
- Do not start the next phase until the user says to.

## How You Ask

- Present available options as a numbered list or selectable choices whenever the interface supports it.
- For each option, briefly explain what it means, why someone might choose it, and its trade-offs.
- Include a recommended option when appropriate, explaining why you recommend it.
- Clearly distinguish facts from assumptions and recommendations.
- Do not ask unnecessary questions when the user has already specified the answer or when the choice has no meaningful impact.
- If clarification is required, pause the affected work until the user responds.

========== FILE: ./.agents/rules/coding-style.md ==========
---
trigger: model_decision
description: "Apply when writing, editing, or reviewing any Python code: naming, PEP 8, type annotations, ruff, logging, and modular design."
---

# Coding Style

## Naming Conventions

- Functions, methods, variables, and modules: use `snake_case`.
- Constants: use `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE`, such as `REMINDER_POLL_MS`.
- Classes: use `PascalCase`, such as `TaskService`.

## Guidelines

- Follow PEP 8 consistently. `ruff check` and `ruff format` are the arbiters; their configuration lives in `pyproject.toml`.
- Prefer focused modules and explicit interfaces.
- Use type annotations for public functions and meaningful data structures.
- Use `pathlib.Path` for paths and the `logging` module instead of `print` (except CLI output).
- Keep I/O, subprocess calls, and platform-specific behavior at clear boundaries.
- Comments explain why, not what. Add a short comment wherever an OS or DBMS concept is being applied (for example, why a transaction or a lock is used).
- Preserve existing conventions when modifying code unless asked to change them.
- Avoid unnecessary renaming that could break compatibility.

# General Project Structure

This will for Constant Update on ProjectF0lderStructure

## Layout

```
plus-ultra/
├── AGENTS.md                  # project instructions (single source of truth)
├── GEMINI.md                  # optional, project root; must not contradict AGENTS.md
├── plan.md                    # phased build plan
├── pyproject.toml
├── README.md
├── .agents/
│   ├── rules/                 # specialized Antigravity rules
│   ├── skills/                # one folder per skill, each with SKILL.md
│   └── workflows/
├── .my/                       # the user's private notes; agents read, never rewrite
├── src/plus_ultra/            # application source
└── tests/                     # pytest tests
```

## Rules

- Keep application source code in `src/plus_ultra/`.
- Keep project instructions in `AGENTS.md` at the project root.
- If `GEMINI.md` is used, keep it at the project root. Antigravity gives it precedence over `AGENTS.md`, so it must not contradict it.
- Keep specialized Antigravity rules in `.agents/rules/`.
- Keep tests in `tests/`, mirroring module names (`repository.py` is tested by `test_repository.py`).
- Keep editor-specific configuration in `.vscode/`.
- Keep documentation in appropriately named Markdown files, such as `README.md`.
- Do not use standard-library module names for files or packages (`code`, `platform`, `queue`, `logging`, `signal`). They shadow the standard library.
- Do not edit anything in `.my/` unless the user asks.
- Do not create directories or files unless the project actually needs them.

========== FILE: ./.agents/rules/init-and-techstack.md ==========

trigger: always_on
description: Enforce the project's locked technology stack and require approval before changing it

# Project Tech Stack Rules

The following technology decisions are locked and are the source of truth for technology choices. Do not replace, remove, or introduce competing technologies without explaining the reason, alternatives, and trade-offs, then asking the user for approval.

If another project instruction (including `GEMINI.md`) conflicts with this file, do not guess which instruction to follow. Treat the conflict as unresolved, explain it to the user, and request approval before making stack-related changes. Do not silently change this file or another instruction file to resolve a conflict.

## Platform
- Target: Ubuntu with GNOME on **Wayland**. Tkinter runs through XWayland, not as a native Wayland client.
- Respect the limits documented in the "GUI limitations (Wayland / XWayland)" section 
- if that file and section exist. If they do not exist, report this gap rather than inventing their contents.
- Do not use X11-only window hacks (`xdotool`, `wmctrl`, `xprop`, raw Xlib calls) without asking. They do not control Wayland-native windows reliably and may not work for this application.

## Tooling
- Use `uv` for Python environment and dependency management.
- Use CPython 3.12.
- The `uv`-managed Python distribution used by this project is expected to bundle Tk. Do not recommend `apt install python3-tk` by default; reassess only if the actual environment demonstrates that Tk is unavailable.

## GUI
- Use Tkinter (`tkinter` and `tkinter.ttk`).
- Tkinter was chosen for simplicity. Do not silently replace it with PySide6, Qt, GTK, or another toolkit.
- Be honest about Wayland/XWayland limitations, especially always-on-top behavior, global positioning, transparency, and window-manager control. Do not promise unsupported behavior; explain constraints and ask before adopting a workaround that changes the approved approach.

## Storage
- Use the Python standard-library `sqlite3` module.
- Do not add an ORM; write SQL directly, with parameterized queries only.
- SQLite is the chosen upgrade from the previous JSON-based storage approach.
- Keep database access behind a clear persistence/data-access layer. Use transactions where multiple related changes must succeed or fail together, and preserve user data during schema changes and migrations.

## System Tray
- Use `pystray` for system-tray integration.
- Use `Pillow` for image handling.
- On Linux, pystray's AppIndicator backend needs system bindings (PyGObject and AppIndicator typelibs) that a plain `uv` virtual environment may not see. If the tray icon does not appear, diagnose and report the actual issue, then stop and follow Change Control. Do not swap the library or install system packages on your own.

## Notifications
- Send desktop notifications by invoking `notify-send` through Python's `subprocess` module, with an argument list and never `shell=True`.
- Check whether `notify-send` is available and handle failure gracefully without crashing the application.
- Do not add another Python notification dependency unless the user approves a change.

## Standard Library
These need no approval: `argparse`, `dataclasses`, `datetime`, `fcntl`, `logging`, `pathlib`, `queue`, `signal`, `sqlite3`, `subprocess`, `threading`, `tkinter`.

## Packaging and Launching
- Do not package this PBL project as a Snap or `.deb` package; that overhead has been explicitly rejected.
- Use `uv run plus-ultra` to launch the application. The only console script is `plus-ultra`.
- Use a `.desktop` entry for autostart.
- Keep autostart paths and commands valid for the actual checkout and `uv` environment; do not hard-code a different user's home directory.

## Development Tools
- Use `ruff` for linting and formatting.
- Use `pytest` for tests.
- Tool configuration lives in `pyproject.toml`.

## Change Control
1. Before implementation, inspect the repository and relevant instructions so these decisions are applied consistently.
2. If a locked choice appears unsuitable or impossible in the actual environment, explain why and present clear alternatives with trade-offs.
3. Ask the user before changing any locked decision. Do not treat a recommendation, convenience, or assumption as approval.
4. Keep project files and dependencies consistent with the approved stack. Do not silently edit `pyproject.toml` or related files to introduce an unapproved alternative.
5. If a change would require editing another instruction file, ask the user first unless the user explicitly requested that specific file be edited.

## Stack Summary
- Ubuntu with GNOME on Wayland; account for Tkinter/XWayland limitations.
- `uv` and CPython 3.12.
- Tkinter/ttk for the GUI.
- Standard-library `sqlite3`, raw parameterized SQL, no ORM.
- `pystray` and Pillow for the system tray.
- `notify-send` invoked through `subprocess` for desktop notifications.
- `ruff` and `pytest` for development.
- Launch with `uv run plus-ultra`; autostart with a `.desktop` entry.
- No Snap or `.deb` packaging.

========== FILE: ./.agents/skills/finish-project-security/readme.md ==========

name: finish-project-security
description: Run the final Gitleaks secret scan when the user explicitly says they are finishing a project or asks for a final security check. Scans current repository files and Git history. Never run automatically on ordinary edits or task completion.
---

# Finish Project Security Check

Only run this check when the user explicitly invokes `/finish-project-security` or directly requests the final security scan.

## Procedure

1. Confirm the workspace root is a Git repository.
2. Run the scanner from the repository root:
   `bash .agents/skills/finish-project-security/scripts/scan-leaked-credentials.sh`

3. Wait for the command to finish and report its actual result.
4. If the exit code is 0, report that both scans completed without detected secrets. Do not claim this proves that no secrets exist.
5. If the exit code is 1, report that potential secrets were detected. Show the reported file paths and line numbers when available, but never print secret values.
6. If the exit code is 2, 124, or another nonzero error, report the check as incomplete or failed. Never describe an incomplete scan as clean.
7. Do not automatically delete files, rewrite Git history, or revoke credentials. Ask before making consequential changes.
8. Do not create unredacted scan reports or paste secret values into chat.
9. Do not continue to publishing or deployment when findings remain unresolved; tell the user what needs review.


========== FILE: ./.agents/skills/finish-project-security/scripts/scan-leaked-credentials.sh ==========

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Final repository secret scan.
# Exit codes:
#   0  = both scans clean
#   1  = secrets detected
#   2  = incomplete/error
#   124 = timeout

set -uo pipefail

readonly SCAN_TIMEOUT=25
readonly GITLEAKS_TIMEOUT=20
readonly FINDINGS_EXIT=23

if ! command -v gitleaks >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "INCOMPLETE: Gitleaks is not installed."
    exit 2
fi

if ! command -v git >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "INCOMPLETE: Git is not installed."
    exit 2
fi

if ! ROOT="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null)"; then
    echo "INCOMPLETE: This directory is not inside a Git repository."
    exit 2
fi

cd "$ROOT" || {
    echo "INCOMPLETE: Cannot enter repository root."
    exit 2
}

TMP_DIR="$(mktemp -d)" || {
    echo "INCOMPLETE: Cannot create temporary scan files."
    exit 2
}
trap 'rm -rf "$TMP_DIR"' EXIT

run_scan() {
    local label="$1"
    shift
    local output="$TMP_DIR/scan.log"
    local status

    : >"$output"

    echo
    echo "=== $label ==="

    timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=2s "${SCAN_TIMEOUT}s" \
        gitleaks "$@" \
        --redact \
        --exit-code "$FINDINGS_EXIT" \
        --timeout "$GITLEAKS_TIMEOUT" \
        >"$output" 2>&1
    status=$?

    # Gitleaks redacts secrets; do not generate or print raw JSON reports.
    cat "$output"

    case "$status" in
        0)
            echo "CLEAN: $label"
            return 0
            ;;
        "$FINDINGS_EXIT")
            echo "FINDINGS: $label detected potential secrets."
            return 1
            ;;
        124|137)
            echo "INCOMPLETE: $label exceeded its timeout."
            return 2
            ;;
        *)
            echo "INCOMPLETE: $label failed (exit code $status)."
            return 2
            ;;
    esac
}

echo "Final security scan"
echo "Repository: $ROOT"
echo "Scope: current files and Git history"
echo "Per-scan hard limit: ${SCAN_TIMEOUT}s"
echo "Maximum normal scan time: about 50s"

overall=0

run_scan "Current files" dir "$ROOT"
status=$?
if (( status == 1 )); then
    overall=1
elif (( status == 2 )); then
    overall=2
fi

# Do not begin another scan if the first one already timed out/failed.
if (( overall != 2 )); then
    run_scan "Git history" git "$ROOT"
    status=$?

    if (( status == 2 )); then
        overall=2
    elif (( status == 1 && overall == 0 )); then
        overall=1
    fi
else
    echo "Git history scan skipped because the current-files scan was incomplete."
fi

echo
case "$overall" in
    0)
        echo "RESULT: CLEAN — both scans completed without detected secrets."
        exit 0
        ;;
    1)
        echo "RESULT: SECRETS DETECTED — review findings before publishing."
        exit 1
        ;;
    *)
        echo "RESULT: INCOMPLETE — do not treat this as a clean scan."
        exit 2
        ;;
esac

========== FILE: ./.agents/workflows/finish-project.md ==========
---
description: Finish a development session by reviewing changes, running appropriate validation, scanning for secrets, and reporting release readiness.
---

# Finish Project

Use this workflow only when I explicitly invoke `/finish-project`.

## Step 1: Review the working tree

- Run `git status --short --branch`.
- Review staged and unstaged changes using safe, read-only Git commands.
- Identify untracked files that belong to the project.
- Never discard, reset, clean, or overwrite my existing work.
- Do not stage, commit, push, publish, or deploy anything without my explicit permission.

## Step 2: Review the changes

- Check for obvious bugs, unfinished code, debugging statements, accidental generated files, and unrelated modifications.
- Check that secrets, credentials, private keys, local configuration, and sensitive data have not been added.
- Do not print secret values in the chat.
- Report concerns and distinguish confirmed problems from possible issues.

## Step 3: Run project validation

- Discover the appropriate test, lint, type-check, and build commands from the project documentation and configuration.
- Run relevant checks that can reasonably finish within the available time.
- Do not install dependencies or make destructive changes without permission.
- Report each check as passed, failed, skipped, or incomplete.
- Never claim that a check passed if it was not run successfully.

## Step 4: use the skill `.agents/skills/finish-project-security`

## Step 5: Report the final status

Summarize:
- Changes reviewed.
- Tests, lint, and build results.
- Secret-scan result.
- Remaining bugs, risks, or incomplete checks.
- Files that may need attention before release.

If secrets are detected or important validation fails, clearly state that the project is not ready for release.

## Step 6: Wait for permission

Do not commit, push, publish, deploy, delete files, or make consequential fixes automatically.

Explain the recommended next steps and wait for my approval before performing those actions.

========== FILE: ./.agents/workflows/start-project.md ==========
---
description: Start a development session by understanding the repository, instructions, Git status, and current priorities
---

# Start Project

Use this workflow when I explicitly invoke `/start-project`.

## Step 1: Understand the repository

- Identify the project root, language, framework, package manager, and overall structure.
- Read the root `GEMINI.md` or `AGENTS.md`, applicable rules, and relevant skill instructions.
- Inspect the README and project-specific setup documentation if present.
- Identify the test, lint, build, and formatting commands from the repository's configuration.

## Step 2: Check Git status

- Run `git status --short --branch`.
- Identify modified, staged, untracked, and ignored files where relevant.
- Never discard, overwrite, reset, or clean existing user changes.
- Do not print credentials or sensitive file contents.

## Step 3: Understand the current state

- Identify the apparent current state of the project.
- Check relevant recent commits when useful.
- Identify incomplete work, failing tests, TODOs, or obvious blockers relevant to the requested task.
- Distinguish confirmed facts from assumptions.

## Step 4: Ask for the task

If I have not yet explained what I want to work on, ask me for the goal.

If my task is already clear, do not ask me to repeat it.

## Step 5: Report readiness

Summarize:
- Project type and architecture.
- Git working-tree status.
- Relevant test and build commands.
- Important blockers or risks.
- Your understanding of the requested task.

Do not modify application files, install packages, or make architectural changes just to inspect the project. Wait for my task if it is not yet specified.

========== FILE: ./.docs/api.md ==========
# PLUS-ULTRA API & Interface Documentation

This document records public APIs, module interfaces, and command-line entry points across each development phase.

---

## 1. CLI Entry Points

### `plus-ultra`
- **Module**: `plus_ultra.main:main`
- **Arguments**:
  - `argv: list[str] | None`: Command line arguments (defaults to `sys.argv[1:]`).
- **Return Code**: `0` on successful execution.
- **Behavior**: Initializes dual logging (`sys.stdout` and `$XDG_STATE_HOME/plus-ultra/launcher.log`), logs startup paths, and exits cleanly.

---

## 2. Implemented Module Specifications (Phase 1)

### `plus_ultra.config`
- `get_data_dir() -> Path`: Resolves `$XDG_DATA_HOME/plus-ultra` (fallback: `~/.local/share/plus-ultra`). Creates directory with mode `0o700`.
- `get_state_dir() -> Path`: Resolves `$XDG_STATE_HOME/plus-ultra` (fallback: `~/.local/state/plus-ultra`). Creates directory with mode `0o700`.
- `get_config_dir() -> Path`: Resolves `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/plus-ultra` (fallback: `~/.config/plus-ultra`). Creates directory with mode `0o700`.
- `get_db_path() -> Path`: Returns `get_data_dir() / "plus-ultra.sqlite3"`.
- `get_log_path() -> Path`: Returns `get_state_dir() / "launcher.log"`.
- `get_user_settings_path() -> Path`: Returns `get_config_dir() / "user-settings.json"`.
- `get_app_version() -> str`: Returns installed package version via `importlib.metadata.version` (fallback `"0.1.0"`).
- `get_metadata() -> dict[str, Any]`: Loads application metadata dictionary (`app_name`, `display_name`, `company`, `description`) from packaged resources.
- **Constants**:
  - `APP_NAME: str = "plus-ultra"`
  - `DB_FILENAME: str = "plus-ultra.sqlite3"`
  - `LOG_FILENAME: str = "launcher.log"`
  - `USER_SETTINGS_FILENAME: str = "user-settings.json"`

### `plus_ultra.main`
- `configure_logging() -> Path`: Sets up dual logging handlers (FileHandler with UTF-8 encoding in state directory, StreamHandler to stdout) with ISO-8601 timestamps. Returns the active log file path.
- `parse_args(args: list[str] | None = None) -> argparse.Namespace`: Parses CLI arguments using packaged metadata.
- `main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> None`: Main application routine.

---

## 3. Implemented Module Specifications (Phase 1b)

### `plus_ultra.settings`
- `load_default_settings() -> dict[str, Any]`: Loads read-only default settings packaged in `plus_ultra.resources/default-settings.json`.
  - Schema keys: `settings_version` (int: 1), `always_on_top` (bool: true), `glow_enabled` (bool: true), `glow_pulse_period_ms` (int: 3000), `reminders_enabled` (bool: true).
- `load_effective_settings() -> dict[str, Any]`: Returns effective settings merging defaults with user overrides from `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/plus-ultra/user-settings.json`.
  - Unknown keys in user file are safely ignored.
  - Keys with mismatched types (strictly checked: `type(val) is type(default_val)`) are ignored, retaining default values.
  - Corrupted JSON files or non-dictionary roots are quarantined to `user-settings.json.bad-<timestamp>` without crashing.
- `update_user_setting(key: str, value: Any) -> dict[str, Any]`: Validates that `key` exists and `type(value) is type(default[key])` (raises `KeyError` or `TypeError` on invalid inputs). Persists overrides atomically via temporary file and POSIX rename (`os.replace`) with mode `0o600`. Returns updated effective settings.
- `reset_setting(key: str) -> None`: Removes user override for `key` and restores default value.
- `reset_all_settings() -> None`: Clears all user overrides, removing the settings file and restoring default settings.
- `reload() -> dict[str, Any]`: Forces an immediate reload from disk, bypassing memory cache.

---

## 4. Implemented Module Specifications (Phase 2)

### `plus_ultra.desktop`
- `SingleInstanceLock(lock_path: Path | None = None)`:
  - `acquire() -> bool`: Attempts to acquire an exclusive, non-blocking advisory file lock (`fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX | LOCK_NB)`) on `$XDG_STATE_HOME/plus-ultra/plus-ultra.lock`. Writes current PID to the file. Returns `True` if acquired, `False` on contention.
  - `release() -> None`: Releases lock (`fcntl.LOCK_UN`), closes file descriptor, and unlinks lock file.
  - Supports Python context manager (`with SingleInstanceLock(): ...`).

### `plus_ultra.ui.theme`
- `CapsuleGeometry`: NamedTuple containing `width: int = 140`, `height: int = 36`, `radius: int = 18`, `top_offset: int = 12`.
- `CapsuleTheme`: NamedTuple containing color tokens:
  - `window_bg: "#12151c"`
  - `idle_fill: "#1a1f2c"`, `idle_border: "#2b3448"`, `idle_text: "#cbd5e1"`
  - `hover_fill: "#242c3d"`, `hover_border: "#00e5ff"`, `hover_text: "#ffffff"`
  - `font_family: "DejaVu Sans"`, `font_size: 9`

### `plus_ultra.ui.floating`
- `FloatingWidget(root: tk.Tk | None, geometry: CapsuleGeometry, theme: CapsuleTheme, on_click_callback: Callable | None, initial_x: int | None = None, initial_y: int | None = None, on_position_change: Callable[[int, int], None] | None = None)`:
  - Initializes a borderless (`overrideredirect(True)`), `-topmost` floating window.
  - Places widget at `(initial_x, initial_y)` if provided, else defaults to top-center of the primary display.
  - Draws a smooth capsule polygon with hover glow animation.
  - Implements mouse drag tracking (`<Button-1>`, `<B1-Motion>`), firing `on_position_change(x, y)` when dragging finishes.
  - Triggers `on_click_callback()` on mouse release when no dragging occurred.
  - `close() -> None`: Closes window and exits main event loop cleanly.
  - `run() -> None`: Starts Tkinter `mainloop()`.

### CLI Flags (`plus-ultra`)
- `--smoke-test`: Automatically initializes database and logging, launches floating widget, and closes after 500ms, returning exit code 0.
- `--no-ui`: Initializes configuration, database schema, state directories, and logging without starting GUI event loop.

---

## 5. Implemented Module Specifications (Phase 3)

### `plus_ultra.db`
- `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION: int = 1`: Version marker for PRAGMA user_version migrations.
- `get_connection(db_path: Path | None = None) -> sqlite3.Connection`:
  - Returns a configured SQLite connection to `db_path` or `$XDG_DATA_HOME/plus-ultra/plus-ultra.sqlite3`.
  - Enables `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;`.
  - Enables `PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;` (Write-Ahead Logging for non-blocking concurrent reads).
  - Sets `PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000;` (5-second lock timeout).
  - Sets `row_factory = sqlite3.Row` for dict-accessible column names.
- `transaction(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> ContextManager[sqlite3.Cursor]`:
  - Context manager guaranteeing ACID transaction atomicity.
  - Commits automatically if the block executes without error.
  - Rolls back immediately on any unhandled exception.
- `get_schema_version(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> int`: Returns current `PRAGMA user_version`.
- `set_schema_version(conn: sqlite3.Connection, version: int) -> None`: Sets `PRAGMA user_version`.
- `init_db(db_path: Path | None = None) -> None`:
  - Runs pending database schema migrations incrementally.
  - Migration 1 creates tables: `categories`, `tasks`, `reminders`, `reminder_fires`, `app_state`, plus partial indexes `idx_categories_name_active` and `idx_reminders_due`.

### `plus_ultra.models`
- Strongly typed `@dataclass(slots=True)` models:
  - `Category`: `id: str`, `name: str`, `color: str = "#4a5568"`, `created_at: str`, `updated_at: str`, `synced_at: str | None`, `is_deleted: int = 0`.
  - `Task`: `id: str`, `title: str`, `category_id: str | None`, `description: str`, `due_date: str | None`, `status: str = "Pending"`, `task_label: str = "College"`, `critical_level: str = "Medium"`, `pace_level: str = "Short-term"`, `is_google_task: bool = False`, `created_at: str`, `updated_at: str`, `synced_at: str | None`, `is_deleted: int = 0`.
  - `Reminder`: `id: str`, `task_id: str`, `remind_at: str`, `created_at: str`, `updated_at: str`, `synced_at: str | None`, `is_deleted: int = 0`.
  - `AppState`: `key: str`, `value: str`.

### `plus_ultra.repository`
- `CategoryRepository(conn: sqlite3.Connection)`:
  - `create(name: str, color: str = "#4a5568") -> Category`: Creates new category. Enforces unique name among active categories.
  - `get_by_id(category_id: str) -> Category | None`: Retrieves category by UUID.
  - `list_active() -> list[Category]`: Lists active (non-deleted) categories ordered alphabetically NOCASE.
  - `update(category_id: str, name: str, color: str) -> Category | None`: Updates category name and color.
  - `soft_delete(category_id: str) -> bool`: Soft-deletes category (`is_deleted = 1`) and cascades `tasks.category_id = NULL` in the same transaction.
- `TaskRepository(conn: sqlite3.Connection)`:
  - `create(...) -> Task`: Creates new task. Validates `title` length > 0, `status`, `critical_level`, and `pace_level`.
  - `get_by_id(task_id: str) -> Task | None`: Retrieves task by UUID.
  - `list_active(category_id: str | None = None, status: str | None = None) -> list[Task]`: Lists active tasks with optional category and status filtering.
  - `update(task_id: str, ...) -> Task | None`: Updates task attributes and automatically updates `updated_at`.
  - `soft_delete(task_id: str) -> bool`: Soft-deletes task and cascades soft-delete to all related reminders (`reminders.is_deleted = 1`) in the same transaction.
- `ReminderRepository(conn: sqlite3.Connection)`:
  - `create(task_id: str, remind_at: str) -> Reminder`: Schedules a reminder for an existing task.
  - `get_by_id(reminder_id: str) -> Reminder | None`: Retrieves reminder by UUID.
  - `list_due(before_timestamp: str) -> list[Reminder]`: Queries active reminders due at or before timestamp, excluding reminders where the parent task was soft-deleted or the reminder was already dispatched on this device.
  - `mark_fired(reminder_id: str) -> None`: Records reminder dispatch in `reminder_fires`.
  - `is_fired(reminder_id: str) -> bool`: Checks if reminder was already dispatched on this device.
  - `soft_delete(reminder_id: str) -> bool`: Soft-deletes an individual reminder.
- `AppStateRepository(conn: sqlite3.Connection)`:
  - `get(key: str, default: str | None = None) -> str | None`: Reads runtime machine state value.
  - `set(key: str, value: str) -> None`: Sets or updates machine state value via upsert (`ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE`).
  - `get_widget_position() -> tuple[int, int] | None`: Reads saved `(widget_x, widget_y)`.
### `plus_ultra.notifications`
- `CapsuleNotification`: Dataclass containing `id: str`, `title: str`, `message: str`, `icon: str`, `action_label: str`, `dismissable: bool`, `created_at: str`, `is_read: bool`.
- `CapsuleNotificationQueue(min_width: int = 160, max_width: int = 440, char_width_px: int = 7)`:
  - `push(title: str, message: str = "", icon: str = "🔔", action_label: str = "View", notification_id: str | None = None) -> CapsuleNotification`: Adds or updates a notification.
  - `dismiss(notification_id: str) -> CapsuleNotification | None`: Dismisses notification by ID.
  - `clear() -> None`: Clears all notifications.
  - `mark_read(notification_id: str) -> None`: Marks a specific notification as read.
  - `mark_all_read() -> None`: Marks all queued notifications as read.
  - `list_all() -> list[CapsuleNotification]`: Returns all notifications.
  - `unread_count: int`: Returns number of unread notifications.
  - `has_unread: bool`: Returns `True` if any unread notification exists.
  - `compute_popup_width(item: CapsuleNotification) -> int`: Returns width for 3-second popup pill clamped between 200px and 360px.

---

## 6. Implemented Module Specifications (Phase 4)

### `plus_ultra.services`
- `VALID_STATUSES`: `("Pending", "In Progress", "Completed", "Overdue")`
- `VALID_CRITICAL_LEVELS`: `("High", "Medium", "Low")`
- `VALID_PACE_LEVELS`: `("Long-term", "Short-term")`
- `TaskService(task_repo: TaskRepository, category_repo: CategoryRepository)`:
  - `create_task(title: str, category_id: str | None = None, description: str = "", due_date: str | None = None, status: str = "Pending", task_label: str = "College", critical_level: str = "Medium", pace_level: str = "Short-term", is_google_task: bool = False) -> Task`: Pure business logic validation; rejects whitespace-only titles, invalid statuses, or non-existent categories.
  - `get_task(task_id: str) -> Task | None`: Retrieves active task.
  - `update_task(task_id: str, ...) -> Task | None`: Validates updates and persists.
  - `toggle_status(task_id: str) -> Task | None`: Cycles status between `Pending` and `Completed`.
  - `soft_delete_task(task_id: str) -> bool`: Soft-deletes task and cascades to reminders.
  - `list_tasks(category_id: str | None = None, status: str | None = None, critical_level: str | None = None, search_query: str | None = None) -> list[Task]`: Multi-criteria querying and case-insensitive text search.
- `CategoryService(category_repo: CategoryRepository)`:
  - `create_category(name: str, color: str = "#4a5568") -> Category`: Validates and creates a new category.
  - `get_category(category_id: str) -> Category | None`: Retrieves category by ID.
  - `update_category(category_id: str, name: str, color: str) -> Category | None`: Updates category name and color.
  - `soft_delete_category(category_id: str) -> bool`: Soft deletes category and nullifies `tasks.category_id` in linked tasks.
  - `list_active() -> list[Category]`: Lists active categories alphabetically.

### `plus_ultra.ui.panel`
- `TaskDialog(parent, categories, task=None, on_save=None, theme=THEME)`:
  - Modal dialog for task creation and editing.
  - Fields: Title, Category combobox, Priority combobox, Pace combobox, Due Date entry, Description/Notes text area.
  - Validates title non-empty and invokes `on_save(data)`.
- `DashboardPanel(parent: tk.Tk, task_service: TaskService, category_service: CategoryService, notification_queue: CapsuleNotificationQueue, on_notifications_cleared: Callable[[], None] | None = None, theme: CapsuleTheme = THEME)`:
  - 3-column workspace window:
    - **Column 1 (Left Sidebar)**: 6 navigation tabs (`Tasks`, `Code`, `Courses`, `Ideas-Doubt`, `Opportunities`, `Quick Links`) + pinned Settings.
    - **Column 2 (Center Panel)**: Option A styled `ttk.Treeview` task list, search bar, status pills (`All`, `Pending`, `Completed`), priority filter combobox, double-click to edit, right-click context menu (toggle status, edit, soft delete), "+ New Task", "+ Category".
    - **Column 3 (Right Sidebar)**: Notification panel with "Mark All Read" and "Clear", wired to `CapsuleNotificationQueue` and capsule bell indicator.
  - `show() -> None`: Displays dashboard window and refreshes data.
  - `hide() -> None`: Hides dashboard window via `withdraw()`.
  - `toggle() -> None`: Toggles visibility between normal and withdrawn.

---

## 7. Implemented Module Specifications (Phase 5)

### `plus_ultra.desktop` (Desktop Notifications)
- `send_desktop_notification(title: str, message: str = "", urgency: str = "normal", timeout_ms: int = 5000, app_name: str = config.APP_NAME) -> bool`:
  - Dispatches native desktop notifications using `notify-send` with an argument list and `shell=False`.
  - Verifies executable availability with `shutil.which`.
  - Catches `SubprocessError` and `OSError` without crashing.
  - Alias: `notify = send_desktop_notification`.

### `plus_ultra.services` (Reminder Management)
- `ReminderService(reminder_repo: ReminderRepository, task_repo: TaskRepository)`:
  - `create_reminder(task_id: str, remind_at: str) -> Reminder`: Validates task existence, active state, and non-empty timestamp.
  - `get_due_reminders(before_timestamp: str) -> list[tuple[Reminder, Task]]`: Queries due reminders, joins with parent active tasks, and filters out already fired or deleted items.
  - `mark_fired(reminder_id: str) -> None`: Records firing in `reminder_fires`.
  - `is_fired(reminder_id: str) -> bool`: Checks if reminder already fired on this device.
  - `soft_delete_reminder(reminder_id: str) -> bool`: Soft-deletes a reminder.

### `plus_ultra.scheduler` (Background Poller)
- `DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_S: float = 10.0`
- `ReminderScheduler(reminder_service, poll_interval_s=10.0, on_reminder_due=None, desktop_notify=True, now_provider=None, db_path=None, conn_factory=None)`:
  - Background daemon thread executing off the Tkinter UI event loop.
  - Thread-isolated SQLite WAL connection to prevent concurrency errors.
  - Producer-consumer dispatch: fires `notify-send` desktop popups and delivers in-app alerts via `on_reminder_due(task, reminder)`.
  - `tick(now_iso: str | None = None, service: ReminderService | None = None) -> list[tuple[Reminder, Task]]`: Evaluates due reminders, invokes notifiers, and marks records fired atomically.
  - `start() -> None`: Starts daemon thread loop.
  - `stop(timeout: float = 2.0) -> None`: Sets `threading.Event` cancellation flag and cleanly joins the thread.
  - `is_running: bool`: Returns `True` if background worker thread is active.

---

## 8. Implemented Module Specifications (Workspace Tabs & Schema v2)

### `plus_ultra.db` (Schema Migration 2)
- `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION: int = 2`
- Tables added:
  - `code_items`: `(id, category, title, content, language, difficulty, is_done, url, notes, created_at, updated_at, synced_at, is_deleted)`
  - `courses`: `(id, title, platform, instructor, total_modules, completed_modules, url, notes, created_at, updated_at, synced_at, is_deleted)`
  - `ideas_doubts`: `(id, kind, title, description, tech_stack_or_ref, status, created_at, updated_at, synced_at, is_deleted)`
  - `opportunities`: `(id, title, organization, kind, url, deadline, status, notes, created_at, updated_at, synced_at, is_deleted)`
  - `quick_links`: `(id, name, target_uri, category, click_count, created_at, updated_at, synced_at, is_deleted)`
- `seed_demo_workspace_data(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None`: Populates rich initial sample items across all tabs if tables are empty.

### `plus_ultra.services`
- `CodeService`:
  - `list_items(category=None, search_query=None) -> list[CodeItem]`
  - `create_item(...) -> CodeItem`
  - `update_code_item(item_id: str, ...) -> bool`
  - `toggle_done(item_id: str) -> bool`
  - `soft_delete_item(item_id: str) -> bool`
- `CourseService`:
  - `list_courses(platform=None, search_query=None) -> list[Course]`
  - `create_course(...) -> Course`
  - `update_course(course_id: str, ...) -> bool`
  - `increment_module(course_id: str) -> bool`
  - `soft_delete_course(course_id: str) -> bool`
- `IdeaDoubtService`:
  - `list_items(kind=None, status=None, search_query=None) -> list[IdeaDoubt]`
  - `create_item(...) -> IdeaDoubt`
  - `update_idea_doubt(item_id: str, ...) -> bool`
  - `advance_status(item_id: str) -> bool`: Cycles through standard stages or resolves doubts.
  - `soft_delete_item(item_id: str) -> bool`
- `OpportunityService`:
  - `list_opportunities(kind=None, status=None, search_query=None) -> list[Opportunity]`
  - `create_opportunity(...) -> Opportunity`
  - `update_opportunity(opp_id: str, ...) -> bool`
  - `advance_status(opp_id: str) -> bool`: Advances Saved -> Applied -> In Progress -> Accepted.
  - `soft_delete_opportunity(opp_id: str) -> bool`
- `QuickLinkService`:
  - `list_links(category=None, search_query=None) -> list[QuickLink]`
  - `create_link(name: str, target_uri: str, category: str) -> QuickLink`
  - `update_quick_link(link_id: str, ...) -> bool`
  - `record_click(link_id: str) -> bool`: Increments click count.
  - `soft_delete_link(link_id: str) -> bool`

### `plus_ultra.ui`
- `CapsuleGeometry`: Doubled dimensions `width=320, height=72, radius=36, top_offset=16`.
- `FloatingWidget`: Double size with 56-point beam, orange bell indicator, and dynamic hide/show coordination.
- `DashboardPanel`:
  - Card-based modern UI replacing table views.
  - Explicit Create, Edit, and Delete modal workflows with fixed-bottom confirmation action bars.
  - Task date and time pickers with optional desktop notification scheduling.
  - Visibility coordination (`on_open`, `on_close`) withdrawing floating pill while open and restoring on close.

## 9. Modular Architecture Subpackages (Architectural Refactoring)

All core modules adhere strictly to single-responsibility decomposition with `<= 500` lines per file.

### `plus_ultra.database`
Subpackage encapsulating SQLite engine, migrations, and seed data:
- `connection.py`:
  - `get_connection(db_path: Path | None = None) -> sqlite3.Connection`: Configures WAL mode, 5s busy timeout, foreign keys, row factory.
  - `transaction(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> ContextManager[sqlite3.Cursor]`: ACID transaction manager with commit/rollback.
- `schema.py`:
  - `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION: int = 2`
  - `SCHEMA_V1_STATEMENTS: tuple[str, ...]`
  - `SCHEMA_V2_STATEMENTS: tuple[str, ...]`
- `migrations.py`:
  - `get_schema_version(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> int`
  - `set_schema_version(conn: sqlite3.Connection, version: int) -> None`
  - `init_db(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None`
- `seeds.py`:
  - `seed_demo_workspace_data(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None`

### `plus_ultra.repositories`
Subpackage containing isolated single-table data access repositories:
- `base.py`: `_now_utc() -> str`
- `CategoryRepository`: CRUD and soft-deletes for categories.
- `TaskRepository`: CRUD, soft-deletes, and filter queries for tasks.
- `ReminderRepository`: CRUD and due-reminder queries.
- `AppStateRepository`: Runtime key-value state persistence (`get_state`, `set_state`).
- `CodeRepository`: CRUD, language filter, and done-toggle for code items.
- `CourseRepository`: CRUD, platform filter, and module progress increments.
- `IdeaDoubtRepository`: CRUD, kind/status filters, and lifecycle advancement.
- `OpportunityRepository`: CRUD, kind/status filters, and application pipeline advancement.
- `QuickLinkRepository`: CRUD, category filter, and click count recording.

### `plus_ultra.services`
Subpackage providing decoupled business logic:
- `TaskService`: Validates constraints, status transitions, search queries.
- `CategoryService`: Manages categories, cascades soft-delete task nullifications.
- `ReminderService`: Enforces reminder creation constraints and atomic firing records.
- `CodeService`: Code snippet search, category filtering, completion toggling.
- `CourseService`: Course module progress incrementation and tracking.
- `IdeaDoubtService`: Idea stage progression and doubt resolution.
- `OpportunityService`: Application pipeline status progression.
- `QuickLinkService`: Link registry and access count updates.

### `plus_ultra.network`
Lightweight network status detection and non-blocking probe utility:
- `is_network_online(host: str = "1.1.1.1", port: int = 53, timeout: float = 1.0) -> bool`: Probes Cloudflare/Google public DNS via lightweight socket connection with zero external dependencies.
- `NetworkStatusMonitor`: Thread-safe background connectivity probe engine using pure Python FIFO queue (`probe_async()`, `get_result() -> bool | None`).

### `plus_ultra.ui.icons`
Pure Tkinter image caching and downsampling utility:
- `find_icon_path(icon_name: str) -> Path | None`: Discovers asset files across `resources/icons`, `resources/base`, `resources/folders`, and `resources/tabs`.
- `get_icon(icon_name: str = "fallback", size: int = 24, master: tk.Misc | None = None) -> tk.PhotoImage | None`: Returns a cached, subsampled `PhotoImage` tied to the active interpreter root with zero third-party dependencies.
- `clear_icon_cache() -> None`: Clears in-memory image references.

### `plus_ultra.ui.components`
Reusable presentation components:
- `DateTimePicker`: Interactive date and time picker featuring `⚡ Now` automatic timestamp insertion, date presets (`Today`, `Tomorrow`, `+3 Days`, `+1 Wk`), time presets (`09:00`, `12:00`, `15:00`, `18:00`, `21:00`, `23:59`), and step adjusters (`-1d`, `+1d`).
- `ScrollableFrame`: Canvas-backed scrollable container with mousewheel bindings.
- `Sidebar`: Draggable navigation sidebar supporting manual cursor drag-and-drop tab reordering, icon-only Settings button on bottom-left, live network status indicator, and renamed tabs (`Tasks`, `Code c-p`, `DSA Tracker`, `Courses`, `PS/I`, `Opportunities`, `Quick Links`).
- `NotificationsPanel`: Focused notification alerts stream with Mark All Read, Clear, and bottom Network Status monitor card with live probe trigger.

### `plus_ultra.ui.dialogs`
Centering modal dialogs with generous dimensions (`680x720`, `680x680`, `640x620`):
- `center_modal(dialog: tk.Toplevel, parent: tk.Misc, width: int, height: int)`
- `TaskDialog`: Supports interactive `DateTimePicker`, priority/pace selectors, and inline category creation directly inside the category dropdown (`+ Add New Category...` and `+` button).
- `OpportunityDialog`: Supports interactive `DateTimePicker` in date-only mode for deadlines.
- `CodeDialog`: Offers predefined language combobox selections (`Python`, `JavaScript`, `TypeScript`, `C`, `C++`, `Java`, `Rust`, `Go`, `Bash`, `SQL`, etc.).
- `CourseDialog`, `IdeaDialog`, `QuickLinkDialog`, `CategoryDialog`.

### `plus_ultra.ui.views`
Decoupled workspace tab views with 2x card sizing and clean badges:
- `TasksView` (`Tasks`): Streamlined view with category filters, priority filters, search bar, and full CRUD.
- `CodeView` (`Code c-p`): Code snippets workspace with language filters and one-click clipboard copy.
- `DSAView` (`DSA Tracker`): Dedicated DSA Problem Tracker view in the main workspace.
- `IdeasView` (`PS/I`): Dual-subtab view featuring dedicated `[ Project Ideas ]` and `[ Concept Doubts ]` tables, each with contextual creation actions (`+ Add Idea` / `+ Add Doubt`).
- `CoursesView`, `OpportunitiesView`, `QuickLinksView`, `SettingsView`.

### `plus_ultra.ui.geometry`
Mathematical capsule coordinate calculations:
- `generate_capsule_points(width, height, radius, steps_per_corner=12) -> list[int]`
- `generate_perimeter_path(width, height, radius, total_points=56) -> list[tuple[float, float]]`



========== FILE: ./.docs/commands.md ==========
# uv

## checking
uv run ruff check .
uv run pytest -v

##
uv run ruff format --check .

## running
uv run python main.py
uv run plus-ultra

## logs

### recent
tail -n 10 ~/.local/state/plus-ultra/launcher.log
### continous
tail -f ~/.local/state/plus-ultra/launcher.log

# Testing Commands

## Automated Test Suites
```bash
# Run all project unit and integration tests
uv run pytest -v

# Run targeted test suites
uv run pytest .tests/test_scheduler.py -v
uv run pytest .tests/test_services.py -v
uv run pytest .tests/test_panel.py -v
uv run pytest .tests/test_db.py -v
uv run pytest .tests/test_repository.py -v
uv run pytest .tests/test_desktop.py -v
uv run pytest .tests/test_ui.py -v
uv run pytest .tests/test_config.py -v
uv run pytest .tests/test_settings.py -v

# Standalone demo data seeding
uv run python .scripts/seeds.py
```

## CLI Test Flags & Headless Validation
```bash
# Smoke test: Launches floating widget, waits 500ms, and closes cleanly (exit 0)
uv run plus-ultra --smoke-test

# Headless check: Initializes paths, logging, and settings without launching GUI
uv run plus-ultra --no-ui

# Headless smoke test in virtual display (CI / remote environments without display)
xvfb-run -a uv run plus-ultra --smoke-test

# Display CLI manual and dynamic metadata
uv run plus-ultra --help
```

## Manual Interactive Desktop Checklist
Run the application interactively in your terminal:
```bash
uv run plus-ultra
```

Verify the following behaviors:
1. **Window Placement**: The capsule appears as a true mathematical pill with zero rectangular dark corners (clipped via XShape).
2. **Animated Border Beam**: A luminous neon cyan beam with a bright tip orbits smoothly around the contour of the pill exclusively on hover (~40 FPS). It stops when unhovered.
3. **Draggability & Position Memory**: Left-click and drag anywhere on the capsule. The widget remembers its coordinate in SQLite `app_state`.
4. **Dashboard Toggle**: Single left-click without dragging toggles the 3-column Dashboard workspace window open and closed.
5. **3-Column Workspace Window (All 6 Tabs + Settings Fully Interactive)**:
   - **Column 1 (Left Sidebar)**: 6 tabs (`Tasks`, `Code`, `Courses`, `Ideas-Doubt`, `Opportunities`, `Quick Links`) + pinned Settings. Modern flat buttons with zero ugly borders or text truncation.
   - **Column 2 (Center Panel)**:
     - **`Tasks`**: Option A styled `ttk.Treeview` task list. `+ New Task`, live search, status pills (`Pending`, `Completed`), and priority filter.
     - **`Code`**: Sub-category filters (`Snippets`, `CLI Commands`, `Syntax Reference`, `DSA Problem Tracker`). Click `📋 Copy` for instant clipboard copy with `✓ Copied!` feedback for 1.5s. Click `○ Todo` / `✓ Solved` to toggle DSA problem status or `🔗 Open Problem` to launch URL.
     - **`Courses`**: Visual progress bars showing completion percentage. Click `+1 Module` to increment module count and auto-update progress in SQLite. Click `🔗 Open` to launch lecture.
     - **`Ideas-Doubt`**: Filter between `💡 Project Ideas` and `❓ Concept Doubts`. Click status buttons (`[Idea]`, `[Planned]`, `[In Progress]`, `[Completed]`, `[Resolved]`) to advance lifecycle.
     - **`Opportunities`**: Role cards with organization, deadlines, and `[Saved]` -> `[Applied]` lifecycle progression. Click `🔗 Apply` to launch URL.
     - **`Quick Links`**: Fast launcher for Web URLs and local folders/files. Click `🚀 Open` to launch default browser or Linux file manager (`xdg-open`), incrementing click count.
     - **`Settings`**: Live toggles for Always-On-Top, local SQLite database diagnostics, and `Reseed Demo Data` button.
   - **Column 3 (Right Sidebar)**: Notification panel showing active alerts. Click `Mark All Read` or `Clear` to clear unread status and remove the orange bell on the floating capsule.

6. **Notification Flow**:
   - Right-click on the capsule to trigger a test notification alert.
   - Main capsule stays compact and displays an orange bell icon (`🔔`).
   - A separate temporary popup pill appears directly below the main capsule for 3 seconds, then cleanly auto-dismisses.
   - Open the dashboard panel, click `Mark All Read` or `Clear`: the orange bell on the capsule disappears and text re-centers.
7. **Background Reminder Evaluation**:
   - Background worker thread periodically polls SQLite every 10 seconds.
   - When a reminder becomes due, it dispatches a native Linux `notify-send` desktop alert and thread-safely enqueues the alert to the floating capsule and dashboard panel without blocking the UI main thread.
8. **Window Stacking**: The capsule stays on top of open windows (`-topmost`).
9. **Single-Instance Enforcement**: Run `uv run plus-ultra` in another terminal; prints `PLUS-ULTRA is already running.` and exits with code 0.
10. **Clean Termination**: `Ctrl+C` in the terminal destroys both windows, stops the scheduler thread, and cleans up the lock file.

========== FILE: ./.docs/data-sources.md ==========
| Variable| Default| What it is for | Analogy |
| ------------------ | ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| `$XDG_DATA_HOME`   | `~/.local/share`   | Important application data that should persist: databases, saved documents, icons, launchers                | A filing cabinet or pantry   |
| `$XDG_STATE_HOME`  | `~/.local/state`   | Data that persists between runs but is okay to lose: logs, history, and “where I left off” information      | A logbook or notepad         |
| `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` | `~/.config`        | User-specific application settings and configuration files                                                  | A settings handbook          |
| `$XDG_CACHE_HOME`  | `~/.cache`         | Rebuildable data that can be regenerated: cached images, downloaded metadata, temporary computation results | A scratchpad                 |
| `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` | `/run/user/1000`\* | Temporary runtime objects for the current login session: sockets, lock files, and other IPC files           | A whiteboard wiped at logout |
|`$XDG_STATE_HOME`| `/plus-ultra/plus-ultra.lock.` | For Lock by storing pid, single instance |
|`log`| `|`$XDG_STATE_HOME`| `~/.local/state/plus-ultra/launcher.log` | For logs realtime |

| `resoucr for internal` | `src/resources` | for internal data |


========== FILE: ./.docs/features.md ==========
# PLUS-ULTRA Features & Tab Specification

This document serves as the single source of truth for dashboard tabs, features, data attributes, and navigation layout.

---

## 1. Dashboard Workspace Tabs (Alphabetical Order)

The sidebar arranges main workspace tabs alphabetically by default, supporting drag-and-drop reordering.

| Tab | Icon | Sub-sections / Scope | Key Attributes & Capabilities |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Code** | 💻 | • Code Snippets<br>• CLI Commands<br>• Syntax Reference<br>• DSA Problem Tracker | • 1-click instant clipboard copy button for snippets and CLI commands.<br>• DSA Problem Tracker: URL link, `is_done` (bool), `your_note` (notes), difficulty, tags. |
| **Courses** | 🎓 | • Course Tracker<br>• Certifications<br>• Tutorials | • Course title, platform/provider, total hours/modules, completed count, progress percentage, lecture links, certificates. |
| **Ideas-Doubt** | 💡 | • Problem Statements<br>• Doubts | • **Problem Statements**: Project/app ideas, description, proposed tech stack, milestones, status (`Idea`, `Planned`, `In Progress`, `Completed`).<br>• **Doubts**: Concept/technical doubts, question notes, references, resolution status (`Open`, `Resolved`). |
| **Opportunities** | 🚀 | • Internships<br>• Jobs<br>• Hackathons<br>• Scholarships | • Role/event title, organization, application URL, deadline, eligibility notes.<br>• Application status: `Saved`, `Applied`, `In Progress`, `Accepted`, `Rejected`. |
| **Quick Links** | ⚡ | • Web URLs<br>• Local OS Files & Folders | • Display name, target URI (`https://...` for browser or `/home/...` for local OS files/folders), category, click count.<br>• 1-click launch via default browser or native Linux file manager (`xdg-open`). |
| **Tasks** | 📌 | • Task List<br>• Categories<br>• Reminders<br>• Micro-Timers | • Replaces old "Daily Drop" naming.<br>• Attributes: `title`, `description`, `due_date`, `status` (`Pending`, `In Progress`, `Completed`, `Overdue`), `category_id`.<br>• **Priority / Metadata**: `task_label` (`Optional`, `College`, `No-use`), `critical_level` (`High`, `Medium`, `Low`), `pace_level` (`Long-term`, `Short-term`).<br>• **Cloud Integration**: `is_google_task` (`bool`, prepared for Google Tasks integration). |
| **Settings & Account** | ⚙️ | Pinned at bottom / avatar modal | • Always-on-top toggle, offline-mode toggle, language.<br>• Supabase cloud sync status (last sync time, manual sync trigger, auth). |

---

## 4. Dashboard Window Layout (3-Column Architecture)

Phase 4 implements a structured 3-column desktop layout:
1. **Left Sidebar (Navigation)**:
   - 6 main workspace tabs arranged alphabetically: `Code`, `Courses`, `Ideas-Doubt`, `Opportunities`, `Quick Links`, `Tasks`.
   - Bottom-pinned Settings & Account controls.
2. **Main Center Panel (Workspace Content)**:
   - Header with active tab title, search bar, and "Add New" button.
   - Filter pills (status, critical level, pace level, categories).
   - Item views (cards/table/snippets) with instant action buttons.
3. **Right Sidebar (Notification Panel)**:
   - Dedicated stream of past and unread reminders, system alerts, and notifications.
   - Unread badge indicator.
   - Actions: "Mark All as Read", "Clear All", and individual notification dismissal.
   - Marking notifications as read automatically clears the orange bell indicator on the floating desktop capsule.

```
┌─────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
│  Left Sidebar   │       Main Center Panel       │    Right Sidebar     │
├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ 1. Code         │ Tab content, search bar,      │ Dedicated            │
│ 2. Courses      │ filter pills, item cards,     │ Notification Panel:  │
│ 3. Ideas-Doubt  │ code snippet copy buttons,    │ • Unread list        │
│ 4. Opportunities│ forms, detail dialogs         │ • Unread badge count │
│ 5. Quick Links  │                               │ • "Mark All as Read" │
│ 6. Tasks        │                               │ • "Clear All"        │
│ ─────────────── │                               │                      │
│ ⚙️ Settings     │                               │ (Clearing unread     │
│                 │                               │ removes orange bell) │
└─────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘

```

## Phase 3: Database Layer & SQLite Persistence

- **Status**: Completed

  - [x] Extended `Task` attributes: `task_label`, `critical_level`, `pace_level`, `is_google_task`.

  - [x] `CategoryRepository`: CRUD, active partial index check, soft-delete cascading nullification (`tasks.category_id = NULL`).
  - [x] `TaskRepository`: CRUD, check constraints, soft-delete cascading to reminders (`reminders.is_deleted = 1`).
  - [x] `ReminderRepository`: CRUD, `list_due()` excluding soft-deleted tasks and fired reminders, `mark_fired()`.
  - [x] `AppStateRepository`: Runtime machine state isolation (`widget_x`, `widget_y`).

  - [x] `FloatingWidget` accepts `initial_x`, `initial_y`, and `on_position_change` callback without DB coupling.

### Issues & Status:
- **Ruff SIM117 on nested context managers**: In tests, combined `with pytest.raises(...), transaction(conn) as cur:` to cleanly test rollbacks and constraint violations.
- **Ruff F841 unused variables**: Resolved by asserting exclusion of future reminder IDs from query results.
- **Strict 80-char line length**: Wrapped all multiline SQL queries, dataclass definitions, and comments to fit strictly within 80 characters.

### Important Notes:
- **Clean Architecture Integrity**: `FloatingWidget` has ZERO database imports. Position persistence is wired purely via high-level callback in `main.py`.
- **Machine State vs Preferences Separation**: User settings (`user-settings.json`, mode `0o600`) hold human choices; machine runtime coordinates (`widget_x`, `widget_y`) live in SQLite `app_state`.
- **Active Name Reusability**: Using SQLite partial unique indexes (`WHERE is_deleted = 0`) allows users to soft-delete a category and create a new category with the same name.
- **Query Planner Verification**: `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` confirms `idx_reminders_due` is actively leveraged for due reminder scans.
- Total test suite now runs 61 tests in 0.56s with 100% pass rate.

## Phase 5: Reminders, Desktop Notifications & Background Scheduler

- **Status**: Completed
- **Date**: 2026-10-16

### Decision D1 (System Tray vs Floating Capsule & Native Notifications):
- Executed tray spike with `pystray._xorg` on Ubuntu GNOME on Wayland.
- Ubuntu GNOME top bar uses the `ubuntu-appindicators` extension (`StatusNotifierItem` DBus interface), while the legacy X11 XEmbed tray has been removed from GNOME.
- User evaluated Decision D1 and approved the recommended option: **Drop the system tray** (`pystray`/`Pillow`) to maintain a 100% pure Python standard library stack with zero external C-binding dependencies, relying on the **Floating Capsule** and **Native Desktop Notifications** (`notify-send`).

### Phase 5 To-do List:
- [x] Implement native desktop notifications in `src/plus_ultra/desktop.py`:
  - [x] `send_desktop_notification(title, message, urgency, timeout_ms, app_name)` invoking `notify-send` via `subprocess.run` with argument list and `shell=False`.
  - [x] Check executable presence via `shutil.which`.
  - [x] Graceful error handling (`SubprocessError`, `OSError`) without crashing.
  - [x] Alias `notify = send_desktop_notification`.
- [x] Implement `ReminderService` in `src/plus_ultra/services.py`:
  - [x] `create_reminder`: validation of task existence and non-empty timestamp.
  - [x] `get_due_reminders`: query due reminders joined with active tasks, excluding fired or deleted reminders.
  - [x] `mark_fired`: atomically record in `reminder_fires`.
  - [x] `is_fired`: check firing state.
  - [x] `soft_delete_reminder`: soft delete reminder.
- [x] Implement `ReminderScheduler` in `src/plus_ultra/scheduler.py`:
  - [x] Dedicated daemon worker thread polling every 10 seconds (`REMINDER_POLL_MS = 10_000`).
  - [x] Thread-isolated SQLite WAL connection to prevent concurrency errors.
  - [x] Producer-consumer pattern: queries SQLite, dispatches `notify-send` desktop alerts, and thread-safely enqueues in-app alerts to the floating capsule and dashboard panel.
  - [x] Controlled cancellation using `threading.Event`.
  - [x] Respects `reminders_enabled` user setting flag.
- [x] Wire integration in `src/plus_ultra/main.py`:
  - [x] Instantiate `ReminderRepository`, `ReminderService`, `ReminderScheduler`.
  - [x] Safe in-app callback enqueuing via `widget.root.after_idle`.
  - [x] Clean shutdown in `_signal_handler` and `finally:` calling `scheduler.stop()`.
  - [x] Support smoke test execution (`scheduler.tick()`).
- [x] Automated test suites:
  - [x] `tests/test_desktop.py`: 3 new unit tests for `send_desktop_notification` (success, missing executable, subprocess error).
  - [x] `tests/test_scheduler.py`: 4 new unit tests (due evaluation, future exclusion, soft-deleted task exclusion, settings disabled, thread lifecycle).
  - [x] `tests/test_services.py`: `test_reminder_service_lifecycle_and_validation` validating constraints and atomic firing.
  - [x] All 78 tests passing via `uv run pytest -v`.
- [x] Verification:
  - [x] Clean linting and formatting via `uv run ruff check .` and `uv run ruff format --check .`.
  - [x] Smoke test passes cleanly via `uv run plus-ultra --smoke-test`.
- [x] Update documentation (`README.md`, `.docs/api.md`, `.docs/architecture.MD`, `.docs/commands.md`, `.docs/progress-log.md`).

### Issues & Status:
- **SQLite check_same_thread concurrency error**: Sharing the main thread's SQLite connection with the background scheduler thread caused `ProgrammingError: SQLite objects created in a thread can only be used in that same thread`. Resolved by opening a dedicated thread-isolated SQLite connection in WAL mode within `ReminderScheduler._run_loop()`, cleanly closing it upon thread exit.
- **Decision D1 C-binding complexity**: `pystray`'s AppIndicator backend required `PyGObject` binary wheels which failed to build without system `pkg-config`/`cairo` dev packages. Settled by dropping `pystray`/`Pillow` in favor of standard-library `subprocess` `notify-send` and the floating capsule.
- **Font Rendering on XWayland**: Tkinter fell back to jagged pixelated bitmap font `fixed` when requesting `DejaVu Sans` because the system's X11 server font database lacked that exact name. Resolved by updating `font_family` to `helvetica`, which maps directly to vector TrueType `nimbus sans l`.
- **Button Outlines and Severe Truncation**: Default Tkinter buttons rendered with 1px raised border and 1px highlight ring, causing harsh white/gray double-borders. Left sidebar (180px) and notification panel (220px) were too narrow. Resolved by setting `bd=0, highlightthickness=0, relief="flat"`, widening the sidebar to 250px, the notification panel to 280px, and default window geometry to 1280x800.
- **Complete CRUD & Guaranteed Visible Confirmation Buttons**: All modal dialogs across all 6 tabs now feature a dedicated, fixed-bottom action bar guaranteeing that `✓ Save [Item]` and `✕ Cancel` buttons are permanently visible regardless of screen size or window manager borders. Full `update` and `delete` operations implemented on repositories, services, and UI.
- **Tasks UI Card Modernization**: Replaced sterile `ttk.Treeview` "SQL table" with a modern card-based layout featuring clickable completion checkboxes, color-coded priority/pace chips, category tags, due date/time badges, and direct `✏️ Edit` and `🗑️ Delete` actions.
- **Task Date & Time Options**: Task creation and editing now includes explicit Due Date and Due Time fields with quick presets (`09:00`, `14:00`, `18:00`, `23:59`) and optional desktop reminder scheduling.
- **Double Capsule Size & Visibility Coordination**: Floating capsule dimensions doubled to `320x72` (radius 36) with scaled-up typography and animated border beam. Capsule automatically hides (`withdraw()`) when the workspace opens and reappears (`deiconify()`) when closed.

### Important Notes:
- **100% Python Standard Library**: Application requires zero external pip packages in production runtime.
- **Thread Safety**: UI calls from the background thread are safely deferred to the Tkinter main thread using `widget.root.after_idle`.
- **Exactly-Once Alert Semantics**: Dispatched reminders are committed to `reminder_fires` table immediately, preventing duplicate alerts across restarts or polling cycles.
- **Interactive Feature Verification**: All 6 tabs (`Tasks`, `Code`, `Courses`, `Ideas-Doubt`, `Opportunities`, `Quick Links`) plus `Settings` are 100% interactive and testable with full CRUD, seeded demo data, and modern card styling.
- Total test suite runs 81 tests with 100% pass rate.

### Issues & Solutions:
- **TclError: image "pyimage2" does not exist in multi-root pytest runs**: `PhotoImage` objects are tied to a specific Tcl interpreter. When pytest destroyed a root window and created a new one, module-level icon caches held stale references. Resolved by keying the icon cache by `(id(active_root), icon_name, size)` and verifying `active_root.tk.call("image", "type", ...)` before returning cached images.
- **SIM114 & Line Length in DSA Filters**: Long chained `if ... or ...` expressions generated line length and ruff violations. Extracted clean predicate methods `_matches_dsa_filter()` and `_matches_filter()` respecting strict `<= 80` character limits.
- **ScrollableFrame attribute consistency**: Fixed view components accessing `.scrollable_frame` to use the canonical `ScrollableFrame.interior` widget.

### Issues & Solutions:
- **Segmentation fault from background thread Tkinter calls**: Calling `after_idle` or `configure` on Tkinter widgets from a secondary background thread during test teardown caused Tcl interpreter memory corruption. Resolved by introducing `NetworkStatusMonitor` with pure Python `queue.Queue`. The background worker thread only communicates via the queue, and the main thread safely polls the queue using `container.after()`.
- **Due entry test compatibility**: Tests in `test_panel.py` directly addressed `dialog.due_entry.insert()`. Resolved by exposing `@property def due_entry` on `TaskDialog` and `OpportunityDialog` delegating directly to `DateTimePicker.entry`.
